- **Order, the owner, 2026-11-16 ([D129](../../decisions.md)):** local first. M3 comes before M2, with step 12's installers ([D134](../../decisions.md)), and step 11 may start now, since step 3 has merged; the M1 path continues. **The owner, 2026-11-21 ([D164](../../decisions.md)):** steps 4 to 7 continue to completion; steps 8, 9 and 10 are held until he reopens the hosted lane, and the exit waits with them.
- **The frozen lane in CI, 2026-11-23 ([D162](../../decisions.md), [#1930](https://github.com/lushly-dev/afd/issues/1930)):** `@lushly-dev/loam-host` and `web`'s hosted criterion are `architecture.json`'s `loam-hosted` lane. For a change, `loam.yml`'s "Hosted development profile" job alone builds, typechecks and tests them, the server's units and P6-E7's `AFD's host path` step included; it runs when a lane path, one of the lane's `inputs` or something the server imports changes. A whole-workspace `pnpm check` (a global file, `check:all`, the daily run) still builds and unit-tests `loam-host`, but not the hosted criterion, which only that job runs: `loam.yml`'s own daily scheduled run opens every gate, so the job checks the lane against `main`'s shell each day. No local job and no local file imports the lane (`pnpm check:boundaries`). While the lane is frozen, fixes that keep that job green are allowed, and steps 4 to 7 continue in it. [Loam's status](../../STATUS.md#the-frozen-hosted-lane) says what a session may do.
- **Proposed 2026-11-16, not yet approved: [10.8](#108-inbound-mcp-and-the-cli-d130-d136-proposed-2026-11-16), inbound MCP and the CLI on the desktop** (D130, D136), with step 11b, P6-D6 and P6-D7, the proposed D137 and the owner's Q3 to Q6; revised after an independent review the same day. Nothing builds it before the coordinator approves it. **Approved by the coordinator on 2026-11-17**, after the owner answered Q3 to Q6 as recommended (D137 to D140); step 11b follows step 11, in M3. ([#1345](https://github.com/lushly-dev/afd/pull/1345) first recorded the answers as one D137 dated 2026-11-16.)
- **Step 1, the host skeleton** ([#932](https://github.com/lushly-dev/afd/pull/932), merged at `784b7e1f`): `@lushly-dev/loam-host` with `createLoamHost` on Hono 4.13.17 and `@hono/node-server` 2.1.6, the `Host` check before the adapter, 3.5's headers, `serveSite`, the data directory and its OS lock, the site written to `public/site`, the sandbox origin, TLS from `[tls]`, `check` and `doctor`'s read-only part; `server.v1.json` and `readServerConfig`; the inventory entry, `loam.yml`'s gates and build, the repository contract; P6-E1's and P6-E2's units so far. It also carries the amendments this specification says step 1 applies: the architecture's sections 4, 6 and 9 and its glossary, phase 1's `secrets` row, A1 to A8 in phase 3's specification, and the notes on D36, D46 and D52. **Deviations, each with its reason:**
  1. **Every response on the shell's origin that is neither a shell page nor a script or style sheet** (`sandbox/proxy.html`, a page or an SVG in an app's package, the projection) is served inert, `sandbox; default-src 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'`: 3.5 names no policy for it, and nothing navigates to one there, since a view's HTML reaches its sandbox as data. The review widened it from HTML to every such response, an SVG being a document too. The sandbox origin still serves `sandbox/` under 3.5's `frame-ancestors <origin>`. **Approved by the coordinator 2026-11-09.**
  2. **A workspace id the hosted server answers at the top level under `<base>`** is refused, `DEPLOYMENT_WORKSPACE_INVALID` on `workspace.id`, the code `readDeployment` gives a reserved id: `api` and `mcp` by `loam-host`, while version 2's schema already refuses `mounts` (its reserved list) and `.well-known` (its pattern admits no dot). `api` covers the default `identity.path` and every `HostModule`, which live under `<base>api/`. **Approved by the coordinator 2026-11-09.**
  3. **`readServerConfig` holds the file-alone rules**, at a new entry `@lushly-dev/loam-manifest/server` that no page imports; what needs the file system, the environment or the deployment file (a secret's value or mode, the sandbox against the deployment's, `[oidc]` against the methods, the kind) is `loam-host`'s `checkHost`, which `check` and `createLoamHost` share. Paths in `loam.server.toml` are read against the file's own directory, as the deployment file's `package` is.
  4. **The registrable domain** in the same-site rule comes from the Public Suffix List through `tldts` 7.4.19, pinned exactly in the manifest (section 2 lists no such dependency; the lockfile already held it through `tough-cookie`), private entries included as browsers read them. The manifest's entry test holds `.` and `/validate` clear of `tldts`, the server reader and every Node.js builtin. **Approved by the coordinator 2026-11-09.**
  5. **`[auth]` and `[mail]` are optional in `server.v1.json`**: a `local` deployment has no provider. Step 3 requires both for a `provider` deployment.
  6. **On Windows, `HOST_SECRET_PERMISSIONS` fails closed**: the ACL read 3.2 names is not built, so every secret file, key and data directory is refused there. The hosted server runs on Linux, macOS and its image. **Approved by the coordinator 2026-11-09 as fail-closed;** a follow-up builds the ACL check. *Resolved 2026-11-13: the ACL check is built (see the follow-up's row in the open items).*
  7. **`<base>api/health`** (3.4) is served from step 1; `<base>mcp`, `<base>api/kernel/call` and `<base>mounts/*` answer 404 with `no-store` until their steps (3.3). A request with no `Host`, two, or an absolute target naming another host is 421 (Node's own 400 for a missing `Host` is turned off so the gate answers it).
  8. **A built page whose policy the header cannot carry as it is** (an entity, a line break, two policy tags, or a `frame-ancestors` or `form-action` of its own, which would win over the header's) refuses the start as `DEPLOYMENT_PATH_INVALID` on `--dist`, the CLI's code for a bad `--dist`.
  9. **Versions 1 and 2 only:** deployment version 3 is not on `main` yet, so `hostKindOf`, `identityModeOf`, `oidcMethodsOf` and `hostRouteSegments` read versions 1 and 2 (`static`, `local`, no methods, 3.4's segments) and gain version 3 when phase 3's step 1 lands.
  10. **Not in step 1:** `modules`, `HostModule` and `HOST_MODULE_RESERVED`, which need the server kernel's `sessionPrincipal` and `callAs` (step 4 at the earliest; step 2's `startDevHost` names `modules`); `doctor`'s database reads and instance probes, which come with the steps that build them. `doctor` never opens `lock.sqlite`, since even its shared read would make a server starting at that moment meet `HOST_DATA_LOCKED`.
  11. **Tests:** the test-only option is a symbol, `SERVE_STATIC_DEPLOYMENT`, kept out of the package's entry; bound ports reach a test through `boundAddresses`, so `LoamHost` keeps 3.1's shape. The lock's own unit is not titled `P6-`, since P6-H1's lock case is step 2's; nor are the units for the `Host` check over a socket, the sandbox's listeners, the in-process `HOST_DATA_LOCKED`, TLS and HSTS, `api/health` and `base_path` serving, which are P6-H1's and P6-H4's criteria and close in steps 2 and 4. The TLS unit makes its certificate with `openssl`, so `suites.mjs` gives `loam.yml` `openssl` a step before step 2. E13 of phase 3 does not exist yet, so `loam-host`'s own unit scans `web/src`, `chrome/src` and `kernel/src` for an import of it.
  12. **A raw `\` in the request line never reaches the host:** Node's HTTP parser answers it `400` before any code runs, so `serveSite`'s `\` refusal (`404`) is proved at its unit, and over a socket the request gets Node's `400` with nothing read. `%5c` reaches the host and is `404`.
- **The independent review of `a1485d78`** ([#932](https://github.com/lushly-dev/afd/pull/932)) said merge after fixes, and found the `Host` check, `serveSite` and the seams sound. Applied:
  - **B1:** a retry of the lock in the same process closed a descriptor to `lock.sqlite`, which on POSIX dropped the lock the process held. A retry is now refused from a set of held directories before it touches the file, and only a file the call created is ever closed.
  - **I1:** the inert policy covers every non-shell response but scripts and style sheets (deviation 1).
  - **I2:** the misfiled `P6-` titles are dropped (deviation 11).
  - **I3:** a failed start leaves nothing half-built. The check's one read of `[tls]` is the one the host serves, so nothing is read once the lock is held, and any failure after the lock releases it. A failed `listen()` closes whatever started and may run again.
  - **I4:** the header unit reads the page through happy-dom, independently, and `sandbox` and `report-uri` join the directives a `<meta>` policy may not hold.
  - **Nits:** `HOST_DIST_INVALID` removed; `:443` normalized away from `Host`; the resolved secrets serialize as their names only; the server reader's admitted keys held equal to `server.v1.json`'s; the manifest's entry test.
- **Step 4 obligation** (the review, 2026-11-09): step 1 matches its few routes on the raw path in one catch-all. Step 4 and step 6 register the MCP route and the relay as prefix routes, and decide how a dot segment or an encoded separator in such a path is matched.
- **For the deployment guide** (step 10): two hosts started at the same moment on one data directory can both be refused `HOST_DATA_LOCKED`, since with a busy timeout of 0 neither waits for the other; start one, then retry.
- **Step 3 obligation** (the coordinator, 2026-11-09): version 4's reserved workspace ids gain `api`, `mcp`, `mounts` and `.well-known`, the configured `identity.path`'s first segment, and every `HostModule` name; step 1's refusal in `loam-host` (deviation 2) then moves into the schema, keeping its code.
- **Step 2, the development profile** ([#945](https://github.com/lushly-dev/afd/pull/945), merged at `c11810fa`): `loam-host dev` in 5.1's order (the lock; the certificate by 5.1's `openssl` command and the generated secret, kept across runs; both listeners bound before the configuration; `dev.json`'s ports, `HOST_PORT_TAKEN` and `HOST_DEV_PORT_CHANGED`; the sandbox on `https://127.0.0.1:<second port>`, the site written with it; the mail sink; `.gitignore` of `*`, files 0600 and directories 0700); `startDevHost` and `scanSecrets` at `@lushly-dev/loam-host/testing`; the harness on a `DevHost` (`launchForDevHost`, and the response CSP read beside the `<meta>`); the `Hosted development profile` job and its `Hosted criterion against the development profile` step; `WORKFLOWS.loam.jobs` and the exit check's recorded run gain it; `suites.mjs` runs `test:hosted`, with `openssl` from step 1; `AGENTS.md`; P6-H1 and P6-H2, with step 1's cases for the `Host` check, the sandbox, the lock in a second process, TLS, health and `base_path`. **Section 14's rows** (approved by the coordinator 2026-11-10): the ceilings are the maximum of three `ubuntu-latest` samples from the `Hosted development profile` job (301, 285 and 283 ms, all 93 MB), plus 25%: 377 ms and 117 MB, which P6-H1 enforces only there (`CI=true`); elsewhere it logs the measurement and asserts the proposed 3,000 ms and 300 MB. (The cold-start row is now the median of five starts under an interim 750 ms: [section 14](#14-budgets), [#1062](https://github.com/lushly-dev/afd/issues/1062).) The development machine (macOS, Apple silicon, under other agents' load) measured 138 to 408 ms and 97 to 98 MB, which is why a single sample's ceiling would flake. **Deviations, each with its reason:**
  1. **A `local` deployment of version 2 is served only through the test-only option until step 3**, which brings version 4. The bin's `dev` refuses one as `HOST_DEPLOYMENT_KIND` (3.3), so P6-H1's "`loam-host dev` from an empty directory" runs through `startDevHost`'s child, which runs the bin's own `runDev` with the option; the bin's case is a unit now and moves to P6-H1 in step 3.
  2. **`dev` takes `--dist`**, defaulting to the workspace's `web/dist`: 5.1's command names none, and the server needs the build it serves.
  3. **The host serves through `@hono/node-server`'s request listener** on servers it creates, or that `dev` hands it already bound (5.1's step 3), rather than through `serve`, which binds as it creates. The gate still runs before the adapter.
  4. **The sandbox's listener is bound when the deployment has a view app or a `[sandbox]` of its own**, since a `[sandbox]` with no listener is `HOST_SANDBOX_INVALID`; 5.1 names the view app.
  5. **`DevHost` gains `data`**, the data directory's path, which P6-H1 and `scanSecrets` read. **`scanSecrets`' `found` names each secret by its index in `known` (or `auth secret`) and its place, never its value**, so a failing assertion cannot print one; it also scans for the profile's generated secret.
  6. **`startDevHost` refuses `organization`, `people`, `resources`, `accessTokenTtlSeconds` and `modules`** until the steps that build them; `db()` opens a database only once it exists (steps 3 and 4), and `journal()` is empty until step 4 writes `journal.jsonl`. The certificate is made before the child starts, so `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` names it from the child's first line.
  7. **`loam-host` no longer has `loam-web` as a dev dependency**, since `web`'s harness now depends on `loam-host`. The header unit reads the web host's page source, whose `<meta>` `vite build` keeps, declared in `scripts/affected/scope.mjs`'s `EXTRA_INPUTS`.
  8. **A fix to step 1:** the gate normalized `:443` away from `Host` but passed the header on unchanged, so the per-socket dispatch missed and answered 404. It now passes on the authority it decided, in `headers` and in `rawHeaders`, from which `@hono/node-server` builds the `Request`; `:443` is dropped only from a host with no other port.
  9. **No new codes for the profile's own refusals** (approved by the coordinator 2026-11-10). A `--data` that is not the profile's, a `dev.json` that does not hold ports, and a missing or failing `openssl` are each `HOST_CONFIG_INVALID`, keyed by the path or by `openssl`, with a suggestion. `dev` takes a data directory only when it is missing, empty, or already the profile's (it holds `dev.json`, or a `.gitignore` of exactly `*`), so it never re-modes or writes into another.
  10. **Unit coverage leaves out two files** (approved by the coordinator 2026-11-10), with the reason in `vitest.config.ts`: `testing/child-launcher.ts`, which spawns the child, and `testing/dev-child.ts`, the child's entry. They run only as the built child, so only the hosted suite reaches them. `startDevHost`'s `DevHost` takes a launcher, and the units drive everything else of it with the profile in-process.
- **The independent review of `bfda381c`** ([#945](https://github.com/lushly-dev/afd/pull/945)) said merge after fixes, with no blocker, and found 5.1's order, the sandbox's site separation, deviation 3 and the testing seams sound. Applied: `Host` rewritten in `rawHeaders` (I1); P6-H1's restart case reopening the shell in a context kept open across it and finding its project (I2); `scanSecrets` reading every file's bytes but the profile's two secret files, and looking for the TLS key's body, with units (I3); the data directory taken only when it is the profile's (I4); section 14's ceilings, enforced only in the hosted job and set from at least three samples (I5); and the nits: `:443` only from a host with no other port, a corrupt `dev.json` refused, `HOST_PORT_TAKEN` naming `--port` or the real `dev.json`, a recorded sandbox port kept, a failed `listen()` closing the host and a retried one adding no second handler, a missing `openssl` named, `basePath` merged into an existing `[site]`, one cached `db()` handle per host, the header test's title, STATUS.md's next steps, `AGENTS.md`'s list of what `pnpm check` does not cover, and the `[::1]` follow-up.
- **Step 0a, AFD's `SqliteAdapter`** ([#1056](https://github.com/lushly-dev/afd/pull/1056), merged at `d152f3dc` on 2026-11-12; written against `main` at `ea0cd027`): `@lushly-dev/local-db/sqlite`, section 4's shape on `node:sqlite`: one table, `records(tbl TEXT NOT NULL, id TEXT NOT NULL, value TEXT NOT NULL, PRIMARY KEY (tbl, id)) WITHOUT ROWID`; `list` through `queryRows`; every write, a batch included, in `BEGIN IMMEDIATE … COMMIT`; WAL with `synchronous = FULL` (D102). P6-E7: `runDataAdapterContract`, every case, under a `P6-E7:` describe; a batch's fault leaving nothing, on disk too; durability across close and reopen; two adapters on one file in one process losing no write; a write past the busy timeout failing as `STORAGE_ERROR` while reads go on under WAL; and `entry.test.ts` holding `/sqlite` out of every other entry. `loam.yml` gains the `AFD's host path` step, `suites.mjs` and the repository contract name it, and a changeset carries `@lushly-dev/local-db`'s minor. Its independent review (2026-11-12) found no blocker; its fixes are in: a refused file is left out of WAL, reads fail as `STORAGE_ERROR`, a connection whose rollback failed is closed, `busyTimeoutMs` is checked, the rollback test throws inside the transaction, and the README names WAL's local filesystem, the blocking wait, UTF-8 ID order, whole-table reads and lone-surrogate IDs. The review's suggested test across two processes is not added: P6-E7's scope is one process. **Deviations, each with its reason:**
  1. **`node:sqlite` is loaded through `process.getBuiltinModule`**, not a static import, so the package's `engines` stay `>=22.12.0` for its other entries and a Node.js without `node:sqlite` gets `STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE` (503) from `/sqlite` rather than a failed import. The README and the entry say it needs 22.13.
  2. **`list()` without `sort` returns ID order**, as `IndexedDbAdapter`'s does, by `ORDER BY id`: SQLite compares UTF-8 bytes where IndexedDB compares UTF-16 code units, so the two differ only between characters above U+FFFF and those from U+E000 to U+FFFF. The contract names no unsorted order.
  3. **A database another program created is refused** (`STORAGE_ERROR`), as `IndexedDbAdapter` refuses one, by `PRAGMA user_version`; the schema is created inside its own `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, so two connections opening a new file create it once.
  4. **`busyTimeoutMs`** (default 5000) bounds how long a write waits for another connection, set by `PRAGMA busy_timeout`, since the constructor's `timeout` arrived in Node.js 22.16; a rollback does not rely on `isTransaction`, also 22.16's.
  5. **Reads run outside a transaction**: each `get` and `list` is one statement on WAL's snapshot.
  6. **The exit check's `LOCATIONS`** do not gain the `AFD's host path` place here: step 7's `--phase 6` reads phase 6's rows and adds it.
- **Step 3, the provider and deployment version 4** (merged in [#1274](https://github.com/lushly-dev/afd/pull/1274) at `6d7c505a`, with its final-check follow-up merged in [#1279](https://github.com/lushly-dev/afd/pull/1279) at `c7c78cbc` on 2026-11-16 UTC): deployment and projection version 4 with `[host] kind` (`deployment.v4.json`, `projection.v4.json`), read as `static` from versions 1 to 3; `api`, `mcp` and `.well-known` join version 4's reserved workspace ids (`mounts` was there), with `DEPLOYMENT_WORKSPACE_INVALID`, so step 1's refusal in `loam-host` is gone (the step 1 obligation; the identity path's first segment was already version 3's `DEPLOYMENT_IDENTITY_INVALID`); `DEPLOYMENT_PROVIDER_NEEDS_HOST` (a warning) and `DEPLOYMENT_DESKTOP_PROVIDER`; `deployment write --host` with `DEPLOYMENT_HOST_MISMATCH`; `workspace-deployment-apply`'s journaled `host: { kind }`; the test-only option removed, P6-H1 and P6-H2 on version 4 files, and the bin's own `dev` in P6-H1. **Better Auth mounted** at `<base><identity.path>` from `createLoamAuthOptions` unchanged, on `<data>/auth.sqlite` (0600, with its journal files) and the mail sink, `GET` and `POST` only, every answer `no-store`; Better Auth loads only for a `provider` deployment. **A7:** the gate deletes every incoming `x-forwarded-for` and `x-loam-client-ip`, in `headers` and `rawHeaders`, and sets `x-loam-client-ip` from the socket; `createLoamAuthOptions` reads only it. **Both forms of `bootstrap`** through `bootstrapOrganization`, under the lock, nothing of it over HTTP; **`start`** with `HOST_NOT_BOOTSTRAPPED` and D95's `DEPLOYMENT_STDIO_TEAM`; `startDevHost`'s `organization` and `people`.

Applied as the coordinator ruled on 2026-11-16, each with a test that fails without it:
    - **B1, Better Auth's environment overrode Loam's trust:** with `BETTER_AUTH_TRUSTED_ORIGINS` set, a foreign origin signed in with `200` and a cookie, and `BETTER_AUTH_SECRETS` replaced the `[auth]` secret. **Decided by the coordinator, as step 3's principle that Loam's safety never rests on the environment:** `check`, `start`, `dev` and `bootstrap` refuse to run while `BETTER_AUTH_TRUSTED_ORIGINS`, `BETTER_AUTH_SECRETS` or `BETTER_AUTH_TELEMETRY` is set (a new code, `HOST_ENV_OVERRIDE`, naming it, with the suggestion to unset it), before writing anything; and `createLoamAuthOptions` passes `secrets: [{ version: 1, value: secret }]` and `telemetry: { enabled: false }` explicitly, so neither comes from the environment if a caller skips the check. The audit of every environment read in Better Auth 1.7.18, its core, passkey and telemetry packages is in phase 3's 5.8: `BETTER_AUTH_TELEMETRY` is refused too, since Better Auth ORs it with the option and would send the deployment's configuration to a third party; the rest are pinned by an option or are not a server trust path. Tests: real-host refusals for each variable through `runDev`, `runBootstrap`, the bin's `check` and `start`, and the data directory left unwritten; the empty variable admitted; a unit that `BETTER_AUTH_SECRETS` does not change the provider's secret.

One window, `main`, created in Rust, navigation held to the shell's origin, its webview profile under the data directory; single instance focuses it; the menus are Rust's ("Command palette", which dispatches Mod+K's key event the shell answers with `palette-open`, "Reload", "Quit", and macOS's edit items). The three commands (10.4) over `mounts.json`, read once, refused unless 0600 and owned (POSIX) or owner-only by its ACL (Windows, `DESKTOP_MOUNTS_PERMISSIONS`); `stdio` framed one message per line with a clean environment, `http` through `reqwest` with no redirects and no proxy, both bounded at 1 MiB out and 4 MiB in, at most four handles per instance (`MOUNT_LIMIT`), every child stopped at exit. `deployment write --host desktop` writes `connect-src 'self' ipc: http://ipc.localhost`, `frame-src` the sandbox scheme's origin when a view app is deployed, and `MountAccess`'s `stdio` entries into `mounts.json` only; the kernel serves `stdio` on the desktop and `shell-info-get` reports `host: 'desktop'`; the web shell's mount port is `DesktopMountTransport` (in the `mount` chunk) when the projection's host is the desktop. `loam-desktop.yml`'s `Desktop on Linux` job, its `suites.mjs` entry, the inventory entry and the repository contract's exception, `.gitignore`'s negation, `WORKFLOWS.desktop`, and `loam.yml`'s gates naming the package.
  - **Decided by the coordinator on 2026-11-16, from the step's stop on two trust rules** (the author's preflight, an independent source review, and Tauri 2.12's source):
    1. **Notifications are a DOM event, not a Tauri event** (10.4, amended): JavaScript's `listen` calls `plugin:event|listen`, which needs `core:event:allow-listen`, forbidden by 10.2 and P6-D4, and Rust's `listen_js` is private; the public `Webview::eval` delivers the event with no permission and no new command. `src-tauri/src/notify.rs` builds the one statement; its units and P6-D2 prove a payload holding `</script>`, U+2028, U+2029 and quotes arrives intact and runs nothing, one over 1 MiB is dropped, and a server request is not delivered.
    2. **P6-D4's "anything else" is every command an app or plugin can register, and Tauri's ACL-exempt framework transport is named and pinned** (19, amended): in 2.12.5 `Webview::on_message` (`webview/mod.rs`) skips the ACL check for `request.cmd != FETCH_CHANNEL_DATA_COMMAND` only, `plugin:__TAURI_CHANNEL__|fetch` (`ipc/channel.rs`), which answers a webview only data queued under that webview's own identifier; Loam uses no `Channel`. `src-tauri/tests/acl.rs` reads the compiled Tauri's source and requires the exempt set to be exactly that one, and that no other file makes an ACL decision; P6-D4 shows the command answering `missing channel id header` from the main frame.

The refusal still holds there, for other reasons: each frame's policy has no IPC source, so its `fetch` to `http://ipc.localhost` is refused, and wry handles only the main frame's web messages, so the fallback never arrives. P6-D4 proves it by effect, not by `typeof`: a `mount_open` counter read from the main frame before and after shows that no frame's `invoke` ran (it goes unanswered). WebKitGTK's behaviour is CI's to show; if a frame's `invoke` runs there, P6-D4 fails and 10.2's origin check follows by amendment.

The runner renders without a GPU (`DRI3 error`), and WebView2 on a desktop passes both with room. **Decided, with the owner's go-ahead, 2026-11-16:** the cold-start ceiling is enforced on Windows alone, and on Linux the measurement is recorded here and in the test's output without failing CI, until step 12 measures each platform (section 14). D52's S1 stays enforced at 50 ms in CI; the SQLite amendment stays open until S1 is measured on a real Linux desktop with a GPU, a STATUS follow-up. A run over 50 ms on the runner is reported, not answered by loosening the ceiling. **Amended 2026-11-17** (the coordinator), after the eighth Linux run's S1 p95 of 54 ms (run `44030535111`; 34 to 67 ms over eight runs, over 50 in four): S1 too is enforced on Windows alone, the Linux figure recorded only, S2 enforced on both; the real-desktop measurement covers S1 and the cold start, and either Linux figure can block the SQLite amendment. **Amended again 2026-11-17** (the owner, [#1408](https://github.com/lushly-dev/afd/issues/1408)): S2 is recorded only on a hosted Linux runner (`CI` set), and enforced everywhere else, Windows and a real Linux desktop included. Its 1,000 separate commits measure the runner's disk flushes: S2 ran 550 to 7,555 ms there on unrelated changes, and failed CI on PRs #1398, #1413 and #1461 and on `main` at `8b54f666`, pausing the factory merge lane; a paired run of four on 2026-11-17 failed both on `main` and passed both on #1398's branch. A median of several runs was not taken, because the slow runs are whole-runner stalls a median of three does not absorb. The test's output still marks S2 "enforced" or "recorded only"; [#1125](https://github.com/lushly-dev/afd/issues/1125) sets real ceilings from about 20 `main` runs' medians, and [#1396](https://github.com/lushly-dev/afd/issues/1396) tracks Windows's first cold start.
  - **Budgets:** first paint unchanged, 92,996 of 93,000 gzip; the kernel's `apps` chunks 395,436 of 395,700 (the `stdio` rule) and `late` within its row (`shell-info-get`'s host); the `mount` chunk 217,899 of 238,268 with the desktop transport in it.
  - **Deviations, each with its reason:** (1) **P6-D2's `stdio` row runs on an AFD fixture, not `hello-mcp`**: `hello-mcp` (Umbel's `serve_mounted`) registers no `afd-help`, so the kernel's discovery refuses it (`MOUNT_NOT_AFD`); making it AFD-discoverable is a change to Umbel's SDK, outside this step (a follow-up). The fixture is a real `createMcpServer` over stdio, and the workflow drops the `endpoints/umbel/**` and `packages/rust/**` triggers that existed for `hello-mcp`. (2) **The Linux job builds no `.deb` or `.AppImage`**: the installers are step 12's ("installers and their sums"). (3) **No `@tauri-apps/cli` or `@tauri-apps/api`**: the app builds with `cargo`, and the shell calls `window.__TAURI_INTERNALS__.invoke`, which is what `@tauri-apps/api`'s `invoke` does; step 12's bundles bring the CLI. (4) **The desktop's sandbox origin is written by the CLI for the platform** (`loam-sandbox://localhost`, or `http://loam-sandbox.localhost` on Windows), whatever `[sandbox]` says; version 4's schemas admit the scheme origin, and the readers only in a desktop file or projection. (5) **A desktop deployment's `stdio` instance has a route** (`mounts/<id>/mcp`), which only names it to the kernel's discovery; the Rust host reaches it by instance. (6) **`mounts.json` on Windows** is created empty, restricted with `icacls` to the user's SID with inheritance removed, then written. (7) **`shell-info-get`'s host is read in stage B**, from the applied projection, so first paint carries none of it; a desktop deployment with no app, which loads no projection in stage B, reports `browser`. (8) **`LOAM_DESKTOP_DATA_DIR`** points the app at another data directory, for the suite; nothing in the webview can set it.

its ceiling is set from the job's samples by a later step with the coordinator (a follow-up); this machine measured 5.7 and 6.0 ms over raw requests under other agents' load, where the SDK client's own `tools/list` read 0.2 ms, so the row is measured over raw HTTP;   - **The trust-boundary review of `68f93075`** (the route and the served list; two independent reviewers from one brief: Claude Opus 5.5 at `high`, no blocker, 2 improvements and 6 nits, "approvable"; GPT-6.2 Astra at `high`, 4 blockers and 2 improvements, "approvable once fixed"). Each fixed, with a unit that fails without it:
    - **A second origin behind a TLS proxy** (Astra): AFD's `allowedOrigins` adds to the origin it derives from `Host` and the socket, which on a plain backend socket is the `http` one. `originAllowed` now admits only the configured origin, or none, on `<base>mcp` and all three metadata documents, before AFD or Better Auth reads the request (403, inert, `no-store` or `no-cache`). Unit: the `http` counterpart, another site and `null` refused; the configured origin and no `Origin` served.
    - **`close()` released the lock under a call in flight** (Astra; Opus's improvement 1): AFD's handler settles when its socket closes, while the command runs on. An outermost middleware tracks every command the handler runs; `close()` refuses new requests (503), waits for those commands, their journal append included, then disposes the server kernel, the handler and the store, and `releaseAll` closes the kernel, the provider and the lock each whatever the one before did. The catch path disposes a kernel it built. Unit: a `project-create` held in its append when `close()` is called; `close()` resolves after the append, `dispose()` runs once, and the entry is in the journal.
    - **The metadata's cache** (Astra): all three documents answer `no-cache` (3.5's "anything else"), A2's no longer carrying Better Auth's public caching. Unit.
    - **The inert policy on the route's own refusals** (Astra): `PUT`, `PATCH`, `HEAD` and `OPTIONS` on `<base>mcp`, `<base>mcp/*`, and a non-`GET` on a metadata path answer 404 with it. Unit.
    - **A fragment's `?`** (Astra's and Opus's nit 5): `rawSearch` takes a query only before any `#`.
    - **The meta-tools** (both): a unit holds that `afd-call`, `afd-batch` and `afd-pipe` of registered commands outside the list (`member-invite`, `layout-set`, `appearance-set`, `workspace-deployment-apply`) answer `COMMAND_NOT_FOUND` and journal nothing, and `afd-discover` lists exactly the served list.
    - **Nits (Opus):** the vacuous copy check dropped (3); `serverPorts` passes the raw remainder to `serveSite`, so its refusals decide, and its errors carry `APP_PACKAGE_INVALID` and a suggestion, with a unit (4); `holdMcpHeaders` covers `appendHeader` (7); `mcp;x`, `//loam/mcp` and the absolute form added to the spellings, the last the same request as its origin form by the gate's rule (8); a throwing close no longer skips the provider or the lock (6).
  - **A focused re-check of `22a3de29`** (a fresh Claude Opus 5.5 at `high`; 2 improvements and 5 nits, "approvable once fixed"), each fixed:
    - **A command could still start after `close()` began** (improvement 1): a batch's or pipeline's next step, or a call the SDK had dispatched, reached the chain after the snapshot. `trackCommands` now refuses any command once it drains (`SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE`, retryable, with a suggestion, before anything runs), and `drain` loops until requests and commands have all settled, bounded by `DRAIN_MS` (10 s, 3.8's shutdown wait), after which `close()` goes on and the closed store refuses what is left. Unit: a held command; one started after the drain refused without running; the drain settling after the held one; a command that never settles giving up at the bound.
    - **The origin test did not reach the proxy case** (improvement 2): the profile serves TLS, so AFD's own check already refused the `http` counterpart there. A unit calls `originAllowed` directly with the proxy's case, a repeated `Origin`, `null`, an empty one, a case variant and a missing `headersDistinct` (which now falls back to the header itself, nit 4).
    - **Nits:** A2's 403 carries the inert policy and `no-cache` (1); `rawSearch` and `holdMcpHeaders`' `appendHeader` and every `writeHead` shape have units (2); this record says the tracking runs first, which reads nothing and leaves `credentialsGate`'s own deletion of a `principal` as it was (3); a failed release in a failed start no longer replaces the start's error (5).

Measured with `CI=true` on this machine, P6-H1's own reading: 196 MB and a 1,228 ms cold start before, 100 MB and 342 to 497 ms after, against step 2's 93 MB on `ubuntu-latest` and 97 to 98 MB here. The row is unchanged.
  - **For phase 3:** the server now holds the deployment's entries, so E8's listed-slug `project-create` over MCP on a gated kernel (phase 3's follow-up of 2026-11-21) can be proved against a hosted deployment with a team instance's `projects`; this step did not add that case. The mounted instance's real `startInstances` refresh still needs step 6's relay.
- **Step 5, the trust-record transport** (written 2026-11-22 against `main` at `01d92259`, merged with `f602b0f9`, on `lush/loam-p6-step5-trust-transport`; merged in [#2055](https://github.com/lushly-dev/afd/pull/2055) at `2b304beb` on 2026-11-22): in a hosted `provider` deployment the browser runs `SERVER_COMMANDS` (`@lushly-dev/loam-kernel`'s `trust-records.ts`, 6.1) on the server and keeps a copy of what the server's records show its person.
  - **The transport (6.2):** `POST <base>api/kernel/call` in `loam-host`'s `kernel-call.ts`, loaded with the server kernel in a `provider` deployment only, so `host.ts`'s static graph is unchanged (P6-H1 100 MB with `CI=true`). In 6.2's order: the same-origin JSON rule, each header sent once, before anything is read (`403 REQUEST_NOT_SAME_ORIGIN`); the body at most `request_body_bytes` (`413`), exactly `{ command, input, scope }` (`400`); the session with `disableCookieCache` (`401 UNAUTHORIZED` or `AGENT_CANNOT_SIGN_IN`; a failed read `503 SERVER_UNAVAILABLE`); the `Loam-Person` header equal to the session's person (amended 6.2: `400` none, `409 SESSION_CHANGED` another); `SERVER_COMMANDS` (`404`) and the deployment's workspace (`400`); a human principal minted from the person's member row read now, run by `KernelParts.callAs` through the palette's chain and retired when the request ends; `200 { result, generation }`, the server's `dev.loam/seq` dropped, and a digest that fails answered as none, so a committed write is never reported retryable. Refusals carry a suggestion, `no-store` and the inert policy; no CORS header. `close()`'s drain waits for it.
  - **The server kernel:** `trust-record-snapshot` (`kernel/src/server/trust-records.ts`, gated kernels only, unjournaled) answers what `project-list { status: 'all' }`, `workspace-app-list` and `agent-member-list { status: 'all' }` answer the caller, from their own handlers, and `generation`, the SHA-256 of their canonical JSON; `persona-list` answers `default` alone (A6). `callAs`, `admitPrincipal`, `retirePrincipal` and `runProviderHook` are built in `principal-gate.ts`'s `serverCalls`, so first paint holds none. **The server's sponsor rule** read kernel-wide membership, `NO_MEMBERS` there, so an owner's `agent-member-add` was always `AGENT_SPONSOR_INVALID`; on a gated kernel `prepare` now reads the roster for the call and the rule decides on it (phase 3 step 7's follow-up on the first human principal).
  - **The copy (6.3), `late-hosted`:** `late/hosted.ts`, loaded through `late/trust-wiring.ts` only when the host gives `serverCall` and the deployment the kernel holds is hosted, or a replay's journal is. Each routed write keeps its definition, so the auth gate, line 0 and the scope middleware run as in phase 3; its handler is the server's call, naming the shell's person, and nothing it does writes the browser's store; it skips the browser's own membership re-read, which the server makes per request. `trust-record-settle` (boot-only, journaled with its input) replaces the three tables and a holder record (`settings`, `loam.trust-records`) in one batch, the agent table with installed rows alone, connected agents held in memory; `agent-member-list` and `-get` answer from the copy. It runs at stage B after the first `session-settle`; after a call whose `generation` differs; on the host's 4.3 refresh through `watchMembership`, when it differs; and after any call that leaves the copy with a person it is not known to be for, once per change. A copy for another person or for no one known is never kept: no snapshot, or one too large, settles it empty. Over 2 MiB or the journal's own bound it is `TRUST_RECORDS_TOO_LARGE`, journaled, shown in the global strip. A6: the agent interface's routed writes are `HUMAN_REQUIRED` in the session's line −1, before any gate or request. A5: routed writes are recorded; a replay follows each period's host from each `workspace-deployment-apply` it replays, so a static period's writes re-run as phase 3's. The browser's own `agent-member-settle` is held on this lane: the server's hook suspends, and the copy follows.
  - **The web host:** `web/src/server-call.ts`, its own chunk, sends the person as `Loam-Person` beside 6.2's body and maps the answers (`200` the result and generation; `401` `UNAUTHORIZED` and `ProviderIdentity.refreshSession()`; `4xx` the body's code; `5xx`, a network failure or a body that is not a result `SERVER_UNAVAILABLE`, retryable). `main.ts` gives the port only when `deployment.json`'s `host.kind` is `hosted` in `provider` mode.
  - **The hook (A4, A8, S4):** `createLoamAuthOptions` takes `onMemberRemoved`; `memberRemoved` (`identity/src/hooks.ts`) ends the person's sessions, grants and connected agents and calls it once, from `afterRemoveMember` (the remover read by the role paths' `before` hook, which has the session) and from `loamServerPlugin`'s new `POST /loam/leave`: the last-owner rule, the revocation, then the rule read again and the row deleted in one transaction, the host told only by the request that deleted it. `/organization/leave` stays in `disabledPaths`. The host's `hearRemovals` and `createRemovalHook` suspend the person's unsuspended agent members in `loam.sqlite` by `agent-member-settle` with `PROVIDER_HOOK_FIELDS`, at most 1,000 a call, tracked by `close()`'s drain; the principal gate admits that mark for that command alone, and boot's mark no longer carries it on the server. The kernel's `member-leave` (human, recorded, membership read again after it) calls the adapter's `organization.leave`, now `POST /loam/leave`.

Kept: the settle handler writes its rows one at a time (older than this step; a follow-up); no unit races two owners' leaves (the transaction holds it). The units the record named but did not have: none for a replay of a static period followed by a hosted one, the digest's failure, the 1,000-agent chunks, `holdSettles`, the shell list's registration or the strip; each is a source trace.
  - **A focused re-check of `6090a4cf`** (a fresh Claude Opus 5.5 at `high`: 3 blockers and 7 improvements, "not approvable"; each blocker reproduced by its own probe). Fixed, each with a unit that fails without it: **a refused settle still made the journal unreplayable** (the input held the oversized snapshot, which the journal truncates): a refusal is now journaled as `{ reason, snapshot: empty, oversized: <bytes> }` (6.3's input gains `oversized`), and the unit replays past it; **the first agent read after another tab's sign-in answered the last person's agents**: `agent-member-list` and `-get` answer none of a copy not known to be this person's, until the settle; **a hosted deployment with no app journaled no apply**, so its replay never read the host: the apply runs for a hosted projection with no app, and stage B names the host when the browser routes and the boot named none, so the replay reads it and does not diverge. Improvements taken: the remover is noted only for a caller who is a member, replaced by the next attempt, and bounded at 64; a `409` runs the session refresh as a `401` does; the copy's agent reads keep phase 3's order (oldest first). Recorded, not changed: in a race of two owners leaving, the one refused `MEMBER_LAST_OWNER` inside the transaction has already lost their sessions (revocation comes first so a failure never leaves a deleted membership with live sessions); the holder names the person when the settle runs, a moment after `refresh` checked them; an embedder that gives a port and no deployment routes by the port alone.

  - **Merged with phase 5's step 4** ([#2058](https://github.com/lushly-dev/afd/pull/2058), `main` at `06dde692`; the coordinator's cross-check of labels and taint through the relay). Labels (5.1) and taint (5.3) are decided in the browser's kernel from the instance's result, which the relay passes back whole and re-serialized, so a `whole` entry's result is content whole and taints the session from its instance, and a `per-part` entry's structured parts are walked as before: the hosted case holds the marks `labelResult` reads (`dev.loam/trust`, `dev.loam/instance`, `dev.loam/structured`) on a call through the relay. One consistency fix, with a unit held to the kernel's `MOUNT_FAILURE_CODES` that fails without it: **the relay's refusals joined `labels.ts`'s `KERNEL_ANSWERS`**, since `mount.ts` carries only their code and the kernel answers in its own words; an instance's own error with such a code keeps `parseMountedResult`'s marks and still taints. **A command the entry exposes to apps alone (`expose.apps`, phase 5's 7.5) stays refused by the relay** (`MOUNT_COMMAND_NOT_EXPOSED`), with a unit: the relay cannot see an app's grant, so admitting it would let a member reach an apps-only command with no grant over a raw request, and phase 5 keeps app calls to mounts off the hosted lane until its milestone C (a follow-up). A copilot running on the server is still phase 5's (P6-X1). **Its review** (a fresh Claude Opus 5.5 at `high` on the resolution: no blocker, "approvable once fixed"): it confirmed no path through `KERNEL_ANSWERS` gets an instance's words or an untainted server result into a session, and that labels hold through the relay; it found the `expose.apps` admission (first written here) widened the relay, which this revision reverts; and it found `MOUNT_UNAUTHORIZED` missing from `KERNEL_ANSWERS` (phase 5's, a follow-up).
  - **Budgets (D115),** against `main` at `2b304beb`: `appsMinified` 444,931, 1,731 over, set to 445,500; first paint 330,692 of 331,000 and the `mount` chunk 219,332 of 238,268, within their rows. On the merge with phase 5's step 4 (`06dde692`), the combined build: `appsMinified` 452,033, set to 452,600; first paint 333,058 of 333,400 and 104,911 gzip of 105,000, the kernel alone 145,888 of 146,300, `late-copilot` 34,931 of 35,500 and `mount` 219,351 of 238,268, each within its row.
- **Step 11b, inbound MCP and the CLI** (written 2026-11-22 against `main` at `663afd52` and merged with `main` at `06dde692`, step 5, #1936 and phase 5's step 4, on `lush/loam-p6-step11b-inbound-mcp`; [#2057](https://github.com/lushly-dev/afd/issues/2057), PR [#2062](https://github.com/lushly-dev/afd/pull/2062)): 10.8 as approved, with phase 5's step 3 amendment.
  - **The Rust host** (`src-tauri/src/inbound.rs`) serves `POST /mcp` on `127.0.0.1` from just before the window is created until it closes or the app exits, over `hyper` 1.12.0 (`server`, `http1`, the version `reqwest` already locked; `httpdate` 1.0.3 is the one new crate). Its checks run in this order, nothing forwarded before the last: one `Host` naming `127.0.0.1:<port>` or `localhost:<port>` (`421`); no `Origin` (`403`); `Authorization: Bearer <token>` in constant time (`401`); the path (`404`); the method (`405`, `Allow: POST`); readiness (`503`); at most 1 MiB, read within 10 s (`413`, `408`); one JSON-RPC request or notification with a string or safe-integer id (`400`). At most 64 connections are open at once, a further one closed before anything is read, with a 10 s header read timeout. A request reaches the main frame as the `loam:inbound` event, its detail `JSON.parse` of one string literal under 10.4's escapes, `{ id, message }` with the host's own id; a notification is `{ message }`, answered `202`. The answer comes back through `inbound_reply` behind `from_shell` (six commands, all registered and granted). None within 60 s, or one over 4 MiB, is `504`; at most 16 wait at once, one more `429`. A page load's `Started` clears readiness and answers every waiting request `503`, and a `ready` until its `Finished` is refused, so the page asks again (`inbound_reply` answers `false`).
  - **`inbound.json`** (`src/connection.rs`) holds `{ url, port, token, pid }`, the token 32 bytes from `getrandom` 0.3.4 in base64url. It is written to a temporary file made mode 0600 with `O_NOFOLLOW` (POSIX) or by `CreateFileW` with this user as owner and a protected one-entry DACL (Windows), checked owner-only on its own handle before the token is written, synced, checked by path, renamed into place and checked again. A volume that keeps neither rule is `DESKTOP_INBOUND_PERMISSIONS` and serves nothing. It is deleted at exit, by the run whose `pid` it names, and `SIGTERM` exits as "Quit".
  - **The bridge** (`src/bridge.rs`, `main.rs`): `loam-desktop mcp` is decided before Tauri starts and attaches to its parent's console on Windows. Before every line it reads the file owner-only and checks the process, so no line goes to a port another program holds since Loam exited; it then sends the line on a new connection and writes the answer as one line. It sends again, once and after reading the file again, only when nothing was delivered (a `401`, or no connection made); an exchange cut after sending is answered "the call may have run", never resent. With no desktop it answers the request's id with `DESKTOP_INBOUND_UNAVAILABLE` ("Open Loam, then retry") and exits 1. `--print-connection` prints the URL and the header line `AFD_HEADERS` reads.
  - **The kernel and the page:** `@lushly-dev/loam-kernel/inbound` holds `DESKTOP_MCP_COMMANDS` and `createInboundDispatcher`, proxies onto `kernel.agent.call` as `loam-inbound` through a direct registry with `interface: 'mcp'`, `createDirectToolSurface()` and the scope field. A pipeline with a failed step is `isError`, as AFD's router marks it, and a handler that throws is answered `-32603`, never left to the 60 s. `KernelParts.inboundCommands()` (`commands.ts`'s `inboundCache`) gives the shell's and every app's own agent-exposed definitions, mounted ones left out. `web/src/inbound.ts` loads in a chunk of its own once stage B has, only where Tauri's IPC is in the page, and says nothing after a failed stage B, so the endpoint keeps answering `503`. `loam-host`'s unit holds that the list contains `HOSTED_MCP_COMMANDS`.
  - **The switch:** `[host] inbound` in version 4's `$defs.host`; `deployment write --host desktop` writes `inbound` to `mounts.json`, which `mounts::read_deployed` reads: off when absent, and no file or a refused one serves nothing.
  - **Readings and deviations, each with its reason:**
    1. **The port is chosen at each start** (bound on port 0) and recorded for the run: 10.8's "kept in `inbound.json`" cannot outlive a file deleted at exit, and the token changes each start anyway.
    2. **The bounds 10.8 does not set:** 16 waiting requests (`429`, as the relay's `MOUNT_LIMIT`), 64 open connections and 10 s to send headers or a body, from the trust review.
    3. **No `_meta` scope is workspace level**, never what the person has open, as on the hosted server.
    4. **`initialize` answers `2024-11-05`**, the revision AFD's own handler answers on its 2025 lane (`packages/server/src/http-handler.ts`).
    5. **`SIGTERM` exits as "Quit"** on POSIX, so a logout or a shutdown deletes `inbound.json` and stops every child; the suites quit the app that way.
    6. **`[host] inbound` is admitted on every kind and written only for the desktop**, the only host with an endpoint.
    7. **An app's own commands include a view app's**, as 10.8's "app commands" reads; only mounted instances' are left out. GPT-6.2 Astra's review read "native only" from the first draft's comments, which said so wrongly, and they are corrected.
    8. **First paint rose 794 bytes and 542 gzip** (330,391 to 331,185 against `663afd52`; 331,750 and 104,567 gzip merged with step 5), against section 14's estimate of 0: `main.ts`'s load of the chunk, and the bindings first paint now exports to it. `vite.config.ts`'s `events` group keeps the direct registry, its engine and the scope field in first paint's own chunks, or Rolldown parts them into three new first-paint chunks; loading the dispatcher from the kernel instead doubled the kernel-alone row, 142 KB to 299 KB. Raised under D115 on the build merged with step 5, #1936 and phase 5's step 4 (`main` at `06dde692`): `jsMinified` to 334,600 (334,043; and P2-E1's own ceiling with it), `jsGzip` to 106,000 (105,541), `kernelMinified` to 146,800 (146,386), and `inboundMinified` new at 39,000 (38,307); `appsMinified` is within phase 5's step 4's row (449,993 of 450,500). Merged with step 6 too (`main` at `c274ddaa`): first paint 334,198 and 105,594 gzip, the kernel alone 146,386 and `apps` 452,358, each within its row (`apps` step 6's 452,600). Merged with phase 3's step 9 too (`main` at `31b409fb`): first paint 334,951 and 105,876 gzip, set to 335,500 and 106,300 under D115, and P2-E1's ceiling with them. `kernel.ts` stays within 1,500 lines (1,499): `KernelParts` extends `commands.ts`'s `InboundParts`. P6-D6's "first paint unchanged" now reads its chunks.
    9. **An id the host issued is sequential**, so trusted page code (D54), which can call the agent interface itself, could also answer one; no frame can (10.2, `tests/origin.rs`).
    10. **Phase 5's labels and taint (step 4, #2058) do not reach an inbound call, by their own design.** They mark a copilot session (5.1, 5.3), through the trace only the copilot's agent port mints, because Loam holds that model's context. An inbound client's model runs outside Loam, so Loam cannot see what entered its context, and an inbound call carries no trace: taint neither marks nor lowers it. 6.1's autonomy still gates every inbound call, and taint's chief lowering, a mounted instance's command, is never served inbound (D138). Whether inbound results should carry 5.1's labels for a client's own use is a follow-up.
    11. **`inbound_reply` answers whether it did anything:** `true` only when an answer reached a request still waiting, `false` for an id never issued, one already answered or a call with neither field, and `ready`'s acceptance (`tests/inbound_reply.rs`, on Tauri's mock runtime). The desktop suite's first CI run found that the review's fix had made it answer `true` for any id.
    12. **P6-D7 needs `@lushly-dev/afd-cli`** as the desktop package's development dependency, so the desktop jobs build it.

  - **The trust review** (the endpoint, `inbound_reply`, the token and the served list). Two independent reviewers had one brief and read, never edited: Claude Opus 5.5 at `high` (1 blocker, 6 improvements, 7 nits, "approvable once fixed") and GPT-6.2 Astra at `high` (10 blockers, 4 improvements, "approvable once fixed"; `git` state checked unchanged after it). Fixed:
    - **Connections before authentication** (Opus B1, Astra's improvement): the connection cap, the header and body timeouts, and a pause after an accept error.
    - **The bridge could send a change twice** (Astra B1, Opus I1): it resends only what was never delivered, on a new connection each time.
    - **A running bridge could reach a stale port** (Astra B2): the file and its process are checked before every line.
    - **The token before the rule was checked** (Astra B3): the handle is checked before any byte is written.
    - **P2-E1's own first-paint ceiling** (Astra B5).
    - **A failed pipeline reported as success** (Astra B6).
    - **`ready` after a failed stage B** (Astra B7).
    - **A stale `ready` across a reload, and a notification dropped with `202`** (Astra B8, Opus N1): the refusal until `Finished`, with the page asking again, and `503`.
    - **`__proto__` in the event's object literal** (Astra B9): `JSON.parse` of a string.
    - **`bytes` pinned exactly** (Astra B10).
    - **A throwing handler left to 504** (Opus I2).
    - **The suites:** `quit` waits for the process itself (Opus I3); the switch tests in a file of their own; `run` resolves on `close` (Opus I4); a signal-ended child (Astra); the refusal test sets its own autonomy; the reload's poller starts before the reload.
    - **The scope test reads the scope itself** (Opus I6); the first-paint wording (Opus I5, deviation 8).
    - **Disputed, by 10.8's text:** Astra B4, view apps' commands (deviation 7). **Not taken:** Opus N3 (the bridge reuses `MOUNT_*` codes for a timeout and a limit, which mean the same), N4 (notifications are not counted against the 16, as only a token holder sends one), N5 (the macOS switch case waits five seconds) and N6 (Windows's rename over a bridge's open file), each a follow-up below, and N7 (the chunk loads with the switch off; it waits for a `ready` the host ignores).
    - **A focused re-check of `f9d1d4e7b`** (a fresh Claude Opus 5.5 at `high`, the fixes alone): every finding closed, no blocker, the dispute right by 10.8, "approvable once fixed". Its improvement is applied: past 300 refused asks (30 s; WKWebView sends no `Finished` for a load that fails after its commit) the page keeps asking every 2 s and reports the delay once. Its nits applied: `inbound_reply` answers `true` with no endpoint, so the page stops asking; the notification comment no longer overclaims; Windows's `quit` tolerates the driver's error on the last window; the macOS `stop()` handles a signal-ended child. Recorded, not changed: an outgoing page's `ready` run after the new page's `Finished` could still open the endpoint before the new listener, ending in a `504` (very unlikely); a connection dropped at the cap is answered "may have run" by the bridge, the safe answer.
    - **A focused check of the merge with `main` at `5b01fe89`** (#1936 and #2030; a fresh Claude Opus 5.5 at `high`, the resolution alone): approvable, no blocker. Its improvement is applied: `test/macos/inbound.test.ts` reads the app's stderr through #2030's `pipeRedactedStderr`, whole lines across chunks, and 10.2's logging bullet names it.

The other stands: an answer over 4 MiB answers `true`, as delivered, though its client gets `504`.
- **Step 12, desktop installers and the updater** (written 2026-11-23 against `main` at `2e29283a`, PR [#2202](https://github.com/lushly-dev/afd/pull/2202); closes [#1462](https://github.com/lushly-dev/afd/issues/1462)): `tauri-plugin-updater` `=2.14.1` (`default-features = false, features = ["rustls-tls", "zip"]`, `Cargo.lock` keeping `tauri-runtime-wry` 2.12.3 and `wry` 0.57.2 untouched) and `src-tauri/src/updater.rs` (Rust-only, no `updater:*` JavaScript permission in `capabilities/main.json`; `tests/origin.rs`, `test/capabilities.test.ts` and `test/macos/barrier.test.ts` refuse `plugin:updater|check`, `download`, `install` and `download_and_install`). Production `updater::start` drives `run_startup` with build-time `LOAM_UPDATER_PUBKEY` and `LOAM_UPDATER_ENDPOINT` (`option_env!` with `cargo:rerun-if-env-changed` in `build.rs`), stays off with zero network requests unless both are non-empty, downloads, cryptographically verifies and installs when enabled, enforces `https://` with a host and no credentials on the feed endpoint, redirects (capped at 10) and the manifest's `download_url` in debug and release builds alike, disables ambient proxies (`no_proxy()`), sets `"requireSignedVersion": true` in `tauri.conf.json`, and never sets `dangerousInsecureTransportProtocol`, `dangerousAcceptInvalidCerts` or `dangerousAcceptInvalidHostnames`. `@tauri-apps/cli` `2.12.4` (exact pin) and desktop icons (`src-tauri/icons/`), `scripts/build-installers.mjs` (`build:installers`) cleaning stale bundles, stripping ambient signing env vars and passing `--no-sign`, staging unsigned `.deb` and `.AppImage` on Linux, `.msi` on Windows, and `.app.tar.gz` and `.dmg` on macOS into `dist-installers/` (gitignored) with `SHA256SUMS.txt` and `test-results/installers-<platform>.json`. `vitest.installers.config.ts` and `test/installers/p6-d5.test.ts` (`test:installers`, P6-D5) verify exact expected installer counts, version match, sizes and SHA-256 sums; initialize `tauri_plugin_updater` and run `updater::start` + `run_startup` without a key to prove zero TCP connections and zero HTTP requests; and, with an ephemeral test CA (`openssl`), a separate plaintext HTTP listener and ephemeral minisign keypairs (`tauri signer`), prove plain HTTP endpoint, plain HTTP download URL, HTTPS->HTTP feed redirect, HTTPS->HTTP artifact redirect, untrusted CA, 1-byte tampered payload, wrong-key signature and wrong-version signature (`requireSignedVersion`) are each refused with the target untouched and zero plaintext connections while a validly signed HTTPS update installs and updates the target on Windows (signed PE launched via `ShellExecuteW` with `/P /UPDATE`), Linux (AppImage ELF) and macOS (`.app` bundle swap). `.github/workflows/loam-desktop.yml` adds `Desktop on Windows` (`windows-latest`, daily and manual, 60 min timeout, recording WebView2 Runtime, Edge and EdgeDriver versions), adds `Build the installers` (`build:installers` + `test:installers`) and `loam-desktop-installers-{linux,windows,macos}` artifact uploads to all three OS jobs, and `test/macos/inbound.test.ts` records the median of 3 fresh-profile macOS WKWebView cold starts to `test-results/cold-start-darwin.json`; `scripts/loam-exit-check.mjs` (`WORKFLOWS.desktop.jobs`) and `scripts/affected/suites.mjs` update to match.

  - **Its units** in `web/src/mount.test.ts` (P6-E5, through the real SDK): a response whose event never ended (no blank line, one `\n`, `\r` or `\r\n`) fails at once, as does each envelope the SDK's strict schema rejects (a scalar, array or `_meta: 5` result, a non-integer or `1e300` code, `method` beside `result`, an extra key); one ended by `\n\n`, `\r\n\r\n` or `\r\r` at the stream's end answers, as does one split across chunks; two calls at once each settle with their own answer; a 4 MiB line in 1 KiB pieces is read in linear time; an instance's own error naming the mark stays `MOUNT_RESULT_INVALID`. The `mount` chunk is 222,887 of 238,268 (D115, measured on `e95b490c`), every other row unchanged.

  - **P6-H7 on one dev host:** `relay.test.ts`'s P6-H7 case now also asks for a recovery link as phase 3's "Can't sign in?" does (`/loam/recover/request`), reads it and the bootstrap's invitation to the first owner from the mail sink, and checks each at the provider (`join/check`, `recover/check`, `200`); its one scan looks for the join and recovery tokens, the MCP access and refresh tokens (`agentToken` asks for `offline_access` and returns the refresh token and its sign-in's cookie values, which are scanned too), the bearers, every session id, and every cookie value (each page's at its sign-in, so a session ended since too, and now) in both its wire and its decoded form. **The two link tokens are expected in `mail.jsonl` and nowhere else:** the sink is the development profile's mail delivery (5.1, 3.7), so the scan, which reads every file of the data directory, finds each there; the row's places (served files, projection, journals, logs, store rows, `localStorage`) hold neither.
  - **P6-H7's "a secret file of mode 0644 refuses the start":** `relay.test.ts`'s last case, `P6-H7: the auth secret at mode 0644 in the data directory refuses the start, HOST_SECRET_PERMISSIONS`, on the same host: `chmod 0644` on `secrets/auth`, then `restart()` rejects with that code and the mode is left as it was. On Windows it grants `BUILTIN\Users` read instead (`icacls`, as the units' `loosen`); that branch has not run. **A product fix it needed:** `dev` and `bootstrap --dev` re-moded an existing auth secret and TLS key to 0600 on every start, so a loosened one was tightened silently and the start never refused. Each is now set exactly 0600 when made (the certificate, no secret, is still made 0600 on every run, as 5.1 says of every file) (a restrictive umask can leave `writeFile`'s mode tighter) and never re-moded, so the host's check refuses a loosened one; a renewal (a missing or unreadable certificate) refuses a loosened key it would replace, `HOST_SECRET_PERMISSIONS` keyed `tls.key_file`, before deleting it. Units in `host/src/dev-files.test.ts` prove each, and the umask case.
  - **P6-H8:** every project-create the journal holds as a success is in the store, the scenario's earlier ones too. The burst runs at most four requests at a time, under way once five creates have succeeded; at least one is outstanding as the SIGKILL is sent, each records whether it was answered, failed before the signal (none may) or was cut by it, and every project id the burst added that the journal does not hold is a distinct cut request's, never one whose create the journal holds. The journal's last line is made partial if the kill left none; AFD's `FileJournalSink`, the server's reader, reads it with that line dropped; after the restart a `project-create` appends after the last whole entry, every line parses, the entries before the crash are unchanged and the `seq`s unbroken.

  - **P6-E5 (Units, `loam-host`):** a `DELETE` for an id the fifth binding evicted is answered `204` with the status, body and headers an unknown id's gets, nothing forwarded, and its use is `MOUNT_SESSION_UNKNOWN`.
  - **P6-E9 (Hosted):** in a hosted `provider` shell nobody has signed in to, opened as a plain page so no harness step runs first, `late-hosted`'s request starts at or after the `loam-stage-b` mark and has its response whole by stage B's end (`kernel.appsReady`, timed by a script the page runs before its own), and a sign-in and a call after it request it no more.
  - **P6-H2 (Hosted):** phase 0's E4 axe runs with the palette open as well as closed, in each theme and density and under forced colors, each case asserting the effective theme and density, and its compact drawer and not-found route (after "Nothing here" shows); the deep link's and the reload's responses carry 3.5's policy, not only the first page's; the first paint's chunks compared by SHA-256, fonts left out as phase 2's E1 leaves them (D159), and a chunk that cannot be read failing the case (they were compared by length, `-1` on either side's failure); Hello's view renders through the sandbox, its `hello-list` answer shown in its frame.
  - **P6-H5 (Hosted):** the refused requests leave the whole of `loam.sqlite` unchanged, row by row, and journal nothing for their commands (only the project count was compared).
  - **P6-H6 (Hosted):** an `application/json` body past `relay_response_bytes` is `502` `MOUNT_RESULT_INVALID`, marked, before any status of the instance's, with none of its body; one under the limit passes.

The exit check also refuses a run that lists a job twice and a flag's value taken out of order, and the plain-pass guard covers P6-E7's `local-db` files. **One deviation:** since D162 the Units place is two steps, and the check counts each half on its own, each row in the halves its test files run in (E2 `loam-host`'s alone, E4 the `loam` job's alone, E1, E3 and E5 both), so a half that drops its suites fails rather than hiding behind the other. The record, its counts and what it does not prove are in [STATUS.md's M1 exit run](../../STATUS.archive-2026-11-phases-3-6-2.md#m1-exit-run-2026-11-23).

- **Follow-ups:** [Follow-ups](#follow-ups).

## What this phase serves

- **Principles:** 3, everything is a command; 7, identity once; 8, agents are first-class users; 13, deterministic core; 15, AFD first; the architecture's "Hosts" and "Distribution" components: browser, Tauri desktop and hosted server with the same chrome and the same kernel; a static site, desktop installers, an OCI image and an MCP registry entry, with deployers hosting it.
- **Decisions it depends on:** D9 (three app kinds); D14 (placement under `endpoints/loam/`, every unit quarantined); D15 (AFD first); D25 (TypeScript first; the desktop is Tauri 2 with the kernel in the webview); D32 (single-tenant); D33 (Tauri mobile reserved); D36 (Hono, with AFD's `createMcpHandler` mounted by Hono's Node adapter); D46 (the collaboration service after phases 2 and 6); D52 (IndexedDB on the desktop, SQLite only on a measurement); D54 (native apps are trusted code); D55 and D56 as narrowed by **D95** (no `stdio` instance in a `provider` workspace); D62 (the sandbox proxy on a second origin); D63 (no secret in the projection); D64 (the same-origin route `mounts/<instance>/mcp`, which "the hosted lane serves in phase 6"); D65 (a mounted instance's exposure); D71 and D78 (scope over MCP and to an instance); D74 (the URL scheme, `index.html` for a path with no file); D84 (phase 3's hosted criterion on phase 6's development profile); D85 to D99, phase 3's, which phase 6 delivers and does not redesign; D98 (a connected agent has at most its person's access).
- **Decisions this specification records:** D100 to D108, decided with this specification's approval on 2026-11-09, in [Amendments](#amendments-to-the-architecture-and-the-decision-log) and the decision log. D100 stays the coordinator's reading of D46, which the owner may overrule.

## Terms

Terms follow the [architecture's glossary](../../architecture.md#12-glossary) and phases 2's and 3's. This specification adds:

| Term | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Hosted server | `@lushly-dev/loam-host`: one Node process serving a deployment's shell, provider, MCP route, trust-record transport and mount relay ([section 3](#3-the-hosted-server-d101)) |
| Server configuration | `loam.server.toml`: what only the server reads, never served: its origin, listener, TLS, data directory, secrets, mail and single sign-on ([3.2](#32-the-server-configuration-loamservertoml-version-1)) |
| Data directory | The server's one directory: its store, journal, provider database, mail sink, written site and lock ([section 4](#4-the-servers-store-and-journal-d102)) |
| Development profile | The hosted server on `https://localhost` with a generated certificate and a mail sink, started by one command ([section 5](#5-the-development-profile-milestone-m1)) |
| Production profile | The hosted server on the deployer's origin, on TLS files or behind a TLS-terminating proxy, with real mail ([section 9](#9-the-production-profile-milestone-m2)) |
| Trust-record command | A command whose records the hosted server holds authoritatively, run there from a browser ([6.1](#61-which-commands-run-on-the-server)) |
| Hosted relay | The real `mounts/<instance>/mcp` route, replacing phase 1's test-only relay ([section 8](#8-the-mount-relay-d105)) |
| Effective command | What a relayed `tools/call` runs at the instance: its tool, or `afd-call`'s `arguments.command` ([8.1](#81-the-route-parse-decide-rebuild)) |
| Milestone | A named exit point inside phase 6 that unblocks something on its own ([section 1](#1-milestones-and-the-order-d100)) |

## 1. Milestones and the order (D100)

### 1.1 Four milestones and the exit

| Milestone | What it delivers | What it unblocks | Steps |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **M1, development profile** | Everything phase 3's 10.2 lists, on `https://localhost`, by one command, with the `Hosted development profile` job, and nothing more | **Phase 3's step 10** (P3-H1 to P3-H5) and **phase 5**'s MCP criterion | 0a, 1 to 7 |
| **M2, hosted lane** | The production profile: the deployer's origin, TLS or a proxy, real mail and single sign-on, limits, `doctor`; the mounted suite through the hosted relay | **The collaboration service's specification** (D100's reading of D46) | 8 |
| **M3, desktop lane** | The same build in Tauri 2 on Linux, Windows and macOS, with mounts over the Rust host | Nothing before phase 7 | 11, 12 |
| **M4, distribution** | The static site documented, the OCI image, installers, the registry entry, and the server card, catalog and guide, built and verified, **nothing published** (Q1) | Nothing before phase 7 | 9, 10, and 12's installers |
| **Exit** | M1 to M4, and the copilot served from the same process once phase 5's runtime exists ([section 12](#12-the-copilot-in-the-same-process)) | **Phase 7** | 13 |

**Why the exit waits for phase 5.** The architecture's exit criterion says the hosted server serves "the copilot from one process", and the copilot runtime is phase 5's. Phase 6 provides the seam; the row that proves it runs when phase 5 has a runtime. Phase 7 needs phases 4, 5 and 6 anyway, so nothing waits longer than it would.

**Why the collaboration service waits for M2, not M1.** This is the coordinator's reading of D46, and the owner may overrule it. The sync endpoint will carry real people's content between real browsers, so its design depends on what only M2 decides: a deployer's origin behind TLS or a proxy, the client address the server trusts, its limits, real mail for the invitations sharing needs, and a store that survives restarts in production. M1 is `https://localhost` with a mail sink, enough for phase 3's and phase 5's criteria and nothing a team runs.

**M1 is the earliest exit point.** Steps 0a, 1 and 2 need nothing of phase 3 and can start at once; steps 3 to 6 follow phase 3's steps as 1.2 lists; step 7 records M1. Nothing in M2, M3 or M4 is on M1's path: the server card, catalog, guide and registry entry are M4's, and the mounted suite against a stock `umbel` through the hosted relay is M2's, while M1 proves 10.2 item 5 against phase 2's scope fixture.

### 1.2 Dependencies on phase 3's steps

Phase 3's plan is in [its section 19](../3-identity/spec.md#19-implementation-plan).

| Phase 6 step | Needs from phase 3 | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0a, SQLite adapter | Nothing | AFD's `local-db` |
| 1, host skeleton | Nothing | Serves `local` deployments of versions 1 to 3 through a test-only option, removed in step 3 |
| 2, development profile | Nothing | HTTPS, certificate, data directory, harness, the new job |
| 3, provider and deployment version 4 | **Step 1** (`createLoamAuthOptions`, `loamServerPlugin`, deployment version 3) and **step 4** (`bootstrapOrganization`; the join and recovery pages) | 10.2 items 1 and 2 |
| 4, server kernel and MCP | **Steps 0a and 0c** (AFD's TypeScript MCP authorization) and **step 7** (`createServerKernel`, `createMcpAuth`, `credentialsGate`, the OAuth provider and its pages, so `getOAuthServerConfig` exists for the root metadata) | 10.2 item 3, and A2 |
| 5, trust-record transport | **Step 7** (`principalGate`'s session source) | 10.2 item 4 |
| 6, hosted relay | **Step 7** (the server's membership read and `decideAccess` version 2) | 10.2 item 5 |
| 7, M1 recorded | Phase 3's step 9's `--phase` option in the exit check, if it has merged; otherwise step 7 adds it and step 9 rebases | 10.2 item 6 |

Phase 3's step 10 needs phase 6's step 7. Phase 3's steps 0b, 2, 5, 6 and 8 are not on phase 6's path.

## 3. The hosted server (D101)

### 3.1 One process

One Node 26 process, `loam-host start` (or `dev`), on Hono 4.13 through `@hono/node-server` 2.1's `serve`, with `node:https`'s `createServer` when the server terminates TLS. Everything a deployment serves is in it: the shell, the provider, the MCP route, the trust-record transport, the hosted relay, the server card, and from phase 5 the copilot's routes. **One process per data directory, and one deployment per process.** No cluster or worker threads: the server kernel is the only writer of its store, as phase 2's `ProjectCache` assumes, and the lock ([section 4](#4-the-servers-store-and-journal-d102)) holds it.

```ts
export interface LoamHostConfig {
	server: ServerConfigV1;                 // read by `readServerConfig` (3.2)
	deployment: string;                     // the path of `loam.deployment.toml`, version 4 with `[host] kind = "hosted"`
	dist: string;                           // the web host's `vite build` output
	modules?: readonly HostModule[];        // phase 5's copilot, and test fixtures (12)
}
export interface HostModule {
	/**
	 * A path segment under `<base_path>api/`. `kernel`, `health` and the first segment of the deployment's
	 * `identity.path` after `api/` (`auth` by default) are reserved; a module naming one refuses construction.
	 */
	name: string;
	routes(app: Hono<{ Bindings: HttpBindings }>, host: HostServices): void;
}
/** Opaque: only `sessionPrincipal` creates one, so a module never builds a principal or holds the kernel. */
export interface ModulePrincipal { readonly person: string; readonly brand: unique symbol }
export interface HostServices {
	/** 6.2's first and third rows, then the principal from the session, as phase 3's 9.5 builds it: always the human. */
	sessionPrincipal(request: Request): Promise<{ ok: true; principal: ModulePrincipal } | { ok: false; response: Response }>;
	/** Runs one command on the server kernel under that principal, through phase 3's principal chain. */
	callAs(principal: ModulePrincipal, command: string, input: unknown, scope: ScopeV1): Promise<CommandResult>;
}
export function createLoamHost(config: LoamHostConfig): Promise<LoamHost>;
export interface LoamHost { readonly origin: string; readonly sandboxOrigin: string | null; listen(): Promise<void>; close(): Promise<void> }
```

`createLoamHost` refuses to construct, with its code ([section 16](#16-failure-modes-and-error-codes)), on any refusal of 3.2 or 3.3, a locked data directory, an unreadable secret, a reserved module name, or a `provider` deployment with a `stdio` instance (D95, `DEPLOYMENT_STDIO_TEAM`). It awaits the server kernel's stage B before reading `mcpOptions()`, which is undefined until `late/` has loaded (`kernel.ts:195`, `:778`). **Only `start`** also refuses a `provider` deployment whose organization was never bootstrapped (`HOST_NOT_BOOTSTRAPPED`, naming `loam-host bootstrap`); `dev` starts, and its shell shows phase 3's `IDENTITY_ORGANIZATION_UNKNOWN` until `bootstrap --dev` runs ([5.1](#51-one-command)).

**Which `host` each kernel reports.** The server kernel reports `host: 'hosted'` and `store: 'sqlite'` in `shell-info-get`; the browser's kernel on a hosted deployment is still `host: 'browser'`, since it runs in a browser.

### 3.2 The server configuration, `loam.server.toml` version 1

The deployment file says what the workspace is and what the browser may know (D63). **What only the server reads goes in a second file**, which phase 3's 2.2 calls "the hosted lane's" configuration: `manifest/schemas/server.v1.json`, read by `readServerConfig`, never served, never in a projection.

```toml
server_version = 1

origin = "https://loam.example.com"   # exact: https, host and port; the deployment's base_path follows it
listen = "127.0.0.1:8443"              # host:port
data = "/var/lib/loam"                 # the data directory (section 4); created 0700

[tls]                                  # absent: plain HTTP, allowed only on a loopback `listen` behind a TLS proxy
cert_file = "/etc/loam/fullchain.pem"  # with [sandbox] on the same `listen`, it must name both hosts
key_file = "/etc/loam/privkey.pem"

[sandbox]                              # needed when the deployment has a view app (D62)
origin = "https://loam-sandbox.example.net"   # another site; must equal the deployment's [sandbox] origin
listen = "127.0.0.1:8444"              # optional; absent, served on `listen` by Host, and [tls]'s certificate names both hosts

[auth]
secret_file = "/etc/loam/auth-secret"  # or secret_env = "LOAM_AUTH_SECRET"; at least 32 bytes

[mail]
transport = "smtp"                     # "sink" (the development profile's) or "smtp"
from = "Acme Loam <loam@example.com>"
smtp_url_env = "LOAM_SMTP_URL"         # or smtp_url_file; smtps:// or smtp:// with STARTTLS required

[oidc.corp]                            # one table per `oidc:<id>` in the deployment's [identity] methods
issuer = "https://login.example.com"
client_id = "loam"
client_secret_file = "/etc/loam/oidc-corp"

[limits]                               # defaults shown
request_body_bytes = 1048576
relay_response_bytes = 4194304
relay_concurrency_per_person = 16
relay_timeout_ms = 60000
relay_session_idle_ms = 900000

[discovery]                            # Q2; defaults shown
server_card = true
ai_catalog = false
agent_guide = false
```

| Refusal | Code |
| --- | --- |
| Unknown key, wrong type, `server_version` not 1 | `HOST_CONFIG_INVALID` |
| `origin` not `https:`, or with a path, query, fragment or userinfo. **Every origin is `https:`**, `localhost` included | `HOST_ORIGIN_INSECURE` |
| No `[tls]` and a `listen` (or `[sandbox] listen`) that is not a loopback address | `HOST_LISTEN_INSECURE` |
| A secret given both ways, neither, an unreadable file, an empty variable, or an auth secret under 32 bytes | `HOST_SECRET_UNAVAILABLE` |
| On POSIX, a secret file, `key_file` or the data directory readable by group or others (on Windows, an ACL granting anyone but the owner) | `HOST_SECRET_PERMISSIONS`, naming the file and `chmod 600` (`700` for the directory); on Windows an `icacls` command that removes inheritance and grants only the current user |
| `[sandbox] origin` present and different from the deployment's, not `https:`, or **the same site** as `origin` (the same registrable domain, or the same host for an IP address or `localhost`) | `HOST_SANDBOX_INVALID` |
| An `oidc:<id>` method with no `[oidc.<id>]`, or a table no method names | `HOST_OIDC_INVALID` |
| `[mail] transport = "sink"` with an `origin` whose host is not `localhost` | `HOST_MAIL_SINK_EXPOSED`: a sink keeps join and recovery links in a file, which only the development profile may do |

**The origin is configured, never derived.** No `X-Forwarded-*` header is read, ever. Every request's `Host` must be `origin`'s host and port, or `[sandbox] origin`'s; any other is `421 Misdirected Request` with nothing run (`HOST_MISDIRECTED`). Better Auth's `baseURL`, the passkey `rpID` and `origin`, the MCP resource, AFD's `allowedHosts` and `allowedOrigins`, and the server card's URL all come from `origin` and `base_path`.

**Why the sandbox is another site.** D62's proxy must not share the shell's cookies or storage partition: a different port on the same host is a different origin but the same site, and `SameSite` cookies, including phase 3's session cookie, are sent to it. A different site is what keeps a view's frame from riding the person's session.

### 3.3 The deployment, version 4
